> **UNIVERSE RETARGETED 2026-08-18, header added 2026-09-05.** Rows opened before 2026-08-18 name organs, venues and capabilities of the retired crypto-exchange desk, and many of those organs have since been deleted. Read an old row as a dated statement of what was missing THEN; a gap whose subject no longer exists is closed by the retirement, not outstanding.
>
> Nothing below is deleted: a row recording what was tried, graded or exhausted on the retired
> desk is exactly the knowledge that stops a future session paying for the same thing twice. But
> it is a RECORD, not a queue. Every new row runs against the MT5/Fusion Markets universe -- FX
> majors/crosses/exotics, metals, equity indices, energy, softs, US share CFDs and the crypto CFDs
> Fusion itself lists. **No crypto-exchange venue may be hunted, screened or scored again**
> (`docs/LAWS.md` S1); crypto reference data is admissible only where a specific reading informs a
> Fusion-executable instrument, never as a universe of its own.

**#130 NEW, LOGGED NOT RANKED (2026-08-23) — the data-source intake pipeline itself never
migrated off crypto.** `docs/research/data_axis_watchlist.md` (3353 lines, 42 catalogued sources)
is almost entirely crypto-exchange-native (Upbit/Bithumb venue state, Kraken historical archive,
CoinGecko taxonomy, Polymarket-vs-Deribit BTC contracts, DEX wallet tape, stablecoin flows) with
essentially zero MT5/FX-macro content (1 incidental grep match repo-wide). `scripts/
source_backlog_next.py --limit 0` currently surfaces exactly these crypto items as "VERIFY this
cycle" / "DECIDE this cycle" — i.e. the desk's own designated tool for "what data source to hunt
next" is actively directing effort at the MT5 UNIVERSAL MANDATE's explicitly banned target
(CLAUDE.md: "no miner, hunter, query... may target crypto-exchange-native opportunities"). Same
defect class as the Hetzner crontab.manifest drift fixed the same day (legacy crypto machinery
still directing live effort post-pivot), just in the research-intake pipeline instead of ops.
NOT fixed here: this needs a genuine per-source re-evaluation against the MT5/FX-macro universe
(42 entries, each requiring real verification work per the watchlist's own stated process), not a
mechanical edit — attempting one blind risks corrupting `libs/research/source_backlog.py`'s
parsing contract for a 3353-line, format-sensitive file. Principal declined the bounded-fix and
full-rebuild options offered in-session in favor of logging this as its own priority item first.
NEXT STEP: a dedicated research pass rebuilding the watchlist against MT5/FX/metals/indices/
energy/commodities mechanisms, source by source, same rigor as the original crypto build.

**THE RANKING LESSON: a guard fed by a rolling window of our OWN activity disarms itself during a
pause — and the pause is usually caused by the thing it guards against.** This re-weights every
row that credits a guard with protection: the guard's *evidence source* is now part of the claim.
It also silently voided half of **R0057**, which deleted the absolute per-8h funding floor on
2026-07-31 reasoning that "the cost gate plus the structural-bleed denylist carry all of its
protection" — one of those two was already a no-op. Encoded as standing probe angle **15 DORMANCY
DISARM** and as `max_audit.check_dormancy_disarm` (governed by L1.40, proven falsifiable:
pre-fix FIRES on both symbols, post-fix silent).

**#1a NEW — R0217 venue-side protective stop is INERT, and it is a REARM blocker.**
`run_cashcarry_executor.py:1015` gates the stop rail on `hasattr(fut, "place_stop_market")`;
`fut` is `binance_testnet` (line 29) and `place_stop_market` is defined **only** in
`binance_live.py`. "The rail that survives host death" returns `[]` unconditionally. Its own
docstring discloses it — disclosed-but-never-gated is an open defect, not documentation. It costs
nothing while the book is flat and becomes live-critical the moment REARM puts capital behind it,
so it is ranked immediately under the REARM ask it gates rather than in the general queue.

**R0206 CLOSES — and it closes by REFUTING its own hypothesis, which moves the carry
investigation rather than ending it.** The proposed mechanism (ranking by funding = ranking by
widest premium ⇒ the sleeve shorts an ongoing squeeze) is **refuted and reversed**: on 164 symbols
2019-09-08..2026-07-31, the top-4-by-funding basis leg is **+0.65 bps/day (t +3.11)** — converging,
mildly *favourable* — and — the non-tautological half — the **basis leg alone** improves
monotonically across funding deciles (d1 −1.89 → d10 +0.61 bps/day), in all four constructions.
(Net by decile also rises, but that is partly mechanical: funding is the ranking variable, so the
funding leg *must* rise with rank. The basis leg carries no such mechanism.) The entry rule is
**exonerated**, so the live −51.74 bps `price_pnl` is redirected to
execution/accounting exactly as the card pre-committed. The gap that remains is the honest
finding and is now rowed as **R0219**: paper-gross +7.77 bps/day vs live −58.27 bps/round-trip is
an **L2.10 reality gap of ~66 bps**, and the next repair hour goes to decomposing it against
`trade_forensics` + `tca`, **not** to re-engineering selection. The +7.77 figure is gross of
costs on a current-universe panel and is recorded as NOT an edge.

_Re-ranked 2026-07-29T06:20Z (REAL re-rank from measured state — one move, and it is a big one).

**#71 (gate-optimality) is ROOT-CAUSED and drops from "blocked on a ruling" to "one YES away",
rising to #1. NEW #87 carries the mechanism.** The blocking framing since 07-26 was that fixing
the gate touches validation-gate strictness, which constitution pt 5 reserves to the principal.
That framing survived four sweeps because the question was only ever asked in ONE direction —
"would this loosen the gate?". Measured this cycle, it is the wrong question. `pbo` and
`reality_check` are **campaign constants**: neither `probability_backtest_overfitting` nor
`whites_reality_check` takes the candidate's own returns as an argument, so every candidate in a
batch receives an identical verdict. On the real 420 campaign, PBO=0.6159 and White RC p=0.4220
forced **420/420 rejections regardless of quality**. And holding 60 pure-noise candidates fixed
while adding one genuine winner to the batch flips the old gates to admit **60/60 pure nulls** —
so the status quo is not strict, it is a batch coin-flip that is *also* unboundedly loose, and
the loose direction opens precisely when the desk starts finding real edge. Fix built, 13 tests
green, thresholds numerically unchanged, production flip NOT self-applied — paged for a YES/NO
(`data/PRINCIPAL_ACTION.md` §1). Ledger `2026-07-29-pbo-rc-are-campaign-constants-not-candidate-gates`.

Everything below retains its 07-28 ranking; the carry unit-economics row (#85) and the cost-rate
brake (#86) remain #2 and #3 — they decide whether the only sleeve is viable, but they cannot run
while the book is deadman-halted, and #71 gates the entire discovery pipeline, which is not._

individual merit, while the per-candidate gates discriminate normally (walk_forward 58.1%,

-- the exact thing TWO_STAGE_DISCOVERY_LAW forbids. A promotion path that cannot promote makes every

  not done outranks work done cheaply.

  catching three wrong numbers in a desk document.

  computed from the desk's own 420-hypothesis record) but the largest work, so it ranks below the
  cheap wins rather than above them.
- **#73 at 18** — cheap, two-sided payoff, and one branch REMOVES a recurring 110k-char-per-seat cost.
- **#74 at 23** — a diagnostic, not a build; ranks on the fact that a silent-failure mode which
  produces success-shaped artifacts corrupts every coverage measure downstream of it.

WHY THE TOP FIVE RANK THERE:
1. **#49 (no client order ID)** — ruin-class AND a hard prerequisite. On an ambiguous timeout the
   desk cannot tell "not placed" from "placed, reply lost", so a retry or restart-and-reconcile
   re-places the order; on a delta-neutral book a duplicated leg IS an unhedged directional
   position — the NOM shape that took -41%. It outranks #2 itself because #2's own 07-31
   no-naked-position invariant is unachievable without idempotent submission: sequencing, not
   preference.
2. **#2 (live connector)** — the gate between a paper book and any compounding at all; principal
   deadline 07-31. Its remaining scope is not features, it is the ruin rails themselves
   (venue-side reduce-only stops at the ruin line, no-naked-position reconcile surviving host
   death, pager de-risk ladder, 6h canary). Every downstream row's value is conditional on it.
3. **#54 (per-venue exposure cap)** — the largest protection-per-hour on the board. SYSTEM_REVIEW
   ranks counterparty concentration as FATAL ("an FTX-class failure is fatal to deployed capital
   regardless of strategy correctness"): a venue failure is a log-wealth zero, and no strategy
   quality defends against it. Re-verified 2026-07-26 that it does not exist (zero hits for
   per_venue|venue_cap|venue_exposure). The fix is a NUMBER. With one venue it binds at 100% and
   changes nothing today — which is precisely why installing it now is nearly free and retrofitting
   it on the day a second venue exists is not.
4. **#41 (spot leg unhedged during futures thrash)** — the only MEASURED money-loser on this
   register: -$1,837.68, fully attributed, concentrated in GTC/SHELL/ONE, with futures realized
   +$13.41 against it. Not a hypothesis, an autopsy. The churn guard (#42) and entry gate (#43)
   removed the trigger; the INVARIANT (spot never left unhedged across a futures re-hedge cycle,
   plus an alert when fut_fills/spot_fills exceeds ~3x) was never built, so recurrence is prevented
   by circumstance rather than by construction.
5. **#37 (orphan-cover market-order path)** — an unbounded, uncapped, un-cooled market-order
   mechanism on the live path: no size cap, no confirm window, no venue-health gate, no
   idempotency, no cooldown. 8+/12 panel models raised it independently. A transient REST desync
   can market-cover into a thin book, and during a real venue outage the cascade is itself a ruin
   path. Ranks below #41 only because #41 already happened.

| 70 | **The literature organ was capped at abstract-level evidence for two runs by a tooling claim that was never re-tested — and it was false** | `docs/research/improvement_inbox.md` #59. Run 3 verified the premise (genuinely no pypdf/fitz/pdfminer/pdftotext/poppler, and `Read` on a local PDF fails because it shells to `pdftoppm`) and then noticed the premise did not imply the conclusion: **PDF text is in FlateDecode streams and the stdlib ships `zlib`.** A ~90-line pure-stdlib extractor reads academic PDFs with zero installs; prototype ran from /tmp, touched no repo file, is NOT committed. EVIDENCE IT MATTERED, immediate and unflattering: the first paper extracted (Hou-Xue-Zhang) **corrected three numbers** the desk had recorded from a search summary one day earlier — actual is 65% fail at t>1.96, **82% at the 2.78 multiple-test hurdle**, worst category **trading frictions 102/106 = 96.2%** (recorded: 64% / "85% at t-cutoff 3" / "liquidity 95 of 102 = 93%"). The recorded prior was NARROWER than the truth. | **BY 2026-08-02.** (1) Land the extractor as `scripts/pdf_text.py` — read-only, no deps, research-lane; source is in improvement_inbox #59. (2) Add an `interior_extracted` field to the literature-card schema so `[ABSTRACT]` and `[PRIMARY]` are distinguishable at the EV gate. (3) **The generalisable defect, which is the real item:** a capability limit was asserted once and inherited verbatim across runs, silently defining what the desk could know. Adopt a standing rule — *any recorded "this box cannot X" carries a re-test date and is retried before it is inherited a second time.* That is the NO-CEILING axiom applied to the desk's own tooling claims, where it demonstrably was not being applied. | brain | 07-26 | open |
| 71 | **GATE-OPTIMALITY: the desk corrects for trial COUNT but not for construction CHOICE — and in crypto the second is the larger error term** | `docs/research/improvement_inbox.md` #60/#61. Four independent literatures converge: (a) Fieberg et al., *Non-standard errors in the cryptocurrency world* (IRFA 2024) crossed 10 design decisions into **20,736 designs** over 43 crypto sorting variables and found non-standard errors *"clearly exceed standard errors"* — i.e. construction variance > sampling variance, and DSR/PBO touch none of it; (b) Chen & Zimmermann vs Hou-Xue-Zhang resolves to a definitional split — **reproduction (same protocol) ~98% success, protocol-robustness ~15-35%** — so "it replicated" almost never means "survives OUR construction"; (c) Jensen-Kelly-Pedersen get high replication by pooling to **13 themes**, meaning the anomaly NAMES are overfit and the themes are not; (d) Andrew Chen shows a literature-derived t-hurdle is **unidentified by construction** (hurdle models must extrapolate into the region publication bias hides), and recommends empirical-Bayes shrinkage + local FDR on the observed right tail instead. **The desk is currently running the most design-fragile protocol (single construction) in the asset class with the largest measured design variance, and calling the output a verdict.** | **BY 2026-08-16.** Four actions, two of them free. (1) FREE, adopt now: the gauntlet asks every literature-sourced candidate *"reproduced under the original protocol, or re-derived under OURS?"* — only the second counts. (2) FREE, adopt now: screening unit becomes the MECHANISM with several constructions pooled under ONE pre-registration, not N screens each burning a multiplicity slot (the desk's own record fits — 420 named price hypotheses died individually; the lone repeat survivor, funding/carry, is a THEME). (3) PILOT the pre-registered design GRID: enumerate defensible construction choices up front, run all, judge the distribution — compatible with §26.3, not a loosening of it, because pre-registration is what stops it becoming the re-roll §26.3 forbids. (4) **The GATE-OPTIMALITY duty's own answer:** do not import a literature t-hurdle; compute shrinkage + local FDR on the desk's OWN logged 420-hypothesis realized right tail, where it IS identified. Also lands the McLean-Pontiff standing haircut (published effects **-58%** post-publication, worse for fat in-sample Sharpes) into `alpha_economics.py` as an explicit multiplier on literature-sourced candidates. | brain | 07-26 | open |
| 72 | **The 13-seat panel filters out its own best findings by construction — and a queued proposal would make it worse** | `docs/research/improvement_inbox.md` #62. *The Cost of Consensus* (arXiv 2605.00914, N=10, R=3, GSM-Hard 1,017 + MMLU-Hard) measures **consensus collapse**: the correct answer was present in the generation pool **53.0%** of the time but team accuracy was **20.7%** — a **32.3pp oracle gap**, with correct→wrong vulnerability up to **70.0%**. Plurality voting discards correct reasoning the panel already produced. **UPDATE 2026-07-26 (literature deep-miner run 3) — ROW #67 MUST SPLIT, and its CM half is now
answered.** #67 bundled two §13 legitimacy rulings (Upbit portal + Coin Metrics) as one principal
decision. **They are not analogous and bundling them risks one bad ruling on both.**
- **Upbit affirmatively PERMITS** *"non-commercial and private purposes such as developing one's own
  strategy and backtesting"* — an express grant covering exactly the desk's use.
- **Coin Metrics grants no such permission.** Operative text recovered (ToU §4.1, byte-stable across
  4 Wayback captures over 18 months, because `coinmetrics.io`'s legal estate now 301s to a marketing
  page after the **Talos acquisition, 2025-07-16** — the terms were removed with **no successor**):
  rights are granted *"solely for **non-commercial internal business purposes**"*. CM's own gloss
  pre-empts the desk's central "but it is purely internal" defence, and NC turns on primary purpose —
  a trading signal has exactly one. All three surfaces (repo LICENSE, live docs, ToU) agree, so there
  is **no looser surface to switch to**, and the repo going stale pushes the desk onto the *more*
  restrictive API path, not a laxer one.
- **⚠️ A SECOND, INDEPENDENT BLOCKER THE DESK HAD NEVER FLAGGED — ToU §6(iii)** bans using the
  Services *"TO INPUT INTO, DEVELOP, TRAIN … OR OTHERWISE USE IN RELATION TO, ANY AI SYSTEM …
  [incl] MACHINE MODELS."* **This is not an NC question, so a favourable NC ruling would not clear
  it — and this desk is an AI system.** A desk of this construction should have found that clause
  first; that it took three runs is itself worth noting.
- **RECOMMENDED RULING: EXCLUDE for any path leading to real capital** (confidence HIGH);
  research-only winding down for what is already done (confidence MODERATE, deliberately not
  self-approved). `data/coinmetrics_flows.jsonl` → **quarantine, do not delete**: it is evidence for a
  completed screen and is the desk's **only pre-2015 BTC price series**; blast radius is one script.
- **THE DESK KEEPS ITS DELIVERABLE.** The negative result — the aggregate exchange-flow / MVRV metric
  class carries no daily-horizon edge over 15 years — is the desk's OWN measurement, not CM content,
  and survives an EXCLUDE ruling intact. The licence costs a feed, not a finding.
- **RECONSTRUCTION IS CHEAPER THAN ASSUMED:** the methodology is fully published by the vendor **with
  a worked $60,000 example** (a free unit test that uses zero CM data), the metric **telescopes into a
  stateless cumulative sum** (~1 week), and the substrate is **AWS Public Blockchain, genesis→today,
  keyless** — whose licence the registry had recorded WRONG *in the desk's favour* (**MIT-0, no
  citation duty**, not "Apache-2.0 + citation"; now corrected). Honest residual: the price leg
  **2010-07-17 → 2011-08-29 (~13.5 months)** has no cleanly-licensed daily source.
- **CHEAPEST UNBLOCK, and it is Creative Commons' own advice: ASK.** One email to Coin Metrics/Talos
  could convert EXCLUDE → ADOPT in a reply. Human action; not sent by any agent.
- **Discipline note worth keeping:** Checkonchain carries the exact series 2009→now with **no licence
  at all** — logged as a temptation **refused**, not a source adopted.

| 93 | **THE HOLM COHORT WAS m=4 WHILE 12 CLOCKS ACCRUED — the only multiplicity control on the only path to capital, running ~3.2x too loose** | Under the two-stage law the backtest gauntlet has **zero promotion authority**, so the Holm-corrected CONCURRENT SLOT COUNT is the entire multiplicity budget protecting Stage-B promotion. That integer was held by three files with three different values: `run_axis_shadows.py:120` `holm_bar(len(_AXES))` → **m=4**; `run_alerts.py` `len([]) + hardcoded 6 + axes` → **10**; `data/shadow_sleeves.json` → `[]`, and that file is a **RUN-ROSTER** of derivative sleeve names (`run_derivative_shadow.py:77-81`), never a cohort registry. Measured truth: **12 clocks accruing** (4 axis + 6 standing + 2 derivative). Applied bar **holm_bar(4)=2.24** against a true **holm_bar(12)=2.64** — α 0.0125/clock vs an intended 0.05/12=0.0042, a realized family-wise error rate **~3.2x design**, and understating m *loosens* the bar, so the error ran in the **phantom-edge direction**. Found and carried unfixed by **three** deep sweeps (07-26 R2b, 07-28 R2, 07-29 R5: *"unmoved for 3 sweeps; every day of delay keeps every clock's Holm bar unverifiable"*) — this is the §37 carry, and it is the class of item that only ever gets fixed by someone deciding to stop carrying it. | **FIXED + VERIFIED LIVE (`9dddc49`).** `libs/research/slot_registry.py` derives the cohort from the clock artifacts and is now the single source; `web/axis_shadows.json` re-read after the change prints `bar=2.64` on all four clocks. The module is not a `len()` — its point is the **fail-safe direction**: an unreadable source marks the cohort `complete=False` (a lower bound) instead of counting zero, and a dormant clock stays counted until RETIRED by an explicit ledgered decision, because over-counting only tightens the bar while under-counting admits noise as edge. 7 tests pin the *direction*, not the arithmetic. **NOT done, deliberately:** the sweeps' literal instruction to "populate `shadow_sleeves.json` from the real inventory" would have corrupted `run_derivative_shadow`'s roster (it would try to run clocks named `cashcarry`/`kimchi_premium`); the cohort truth lives in `data/forward_slots.json` and the roster keeps its contract. **CONSEQUENCE THE WRONG COUNT WAS HIDING:** the cohort is **exactly 12/12, at the law's cap** — not 10/12 with two free slots. None of the 44 `SCREEN-INTERESTING` results from the 07-26 axis screens can be enrolled without an **EV-eviction** (R0046, due 08-06). Ledger `2026-07-30-holm-cohort-was-m4-while-12-clocks-accrued`; F0005. | brain | 07-30 | fixed-verified |

_Re-ranked 2026-07-30T07:35Z. **One move, and one deliberate un-move.** **#93 enters at the top**: every other open row costs discovery latency or mis-ordered candidates, but #93 is the only one that could have sized real capital on a phantom — it governs Stage-B, which is the sole promotion path, and it was running 3.2x loose. Fixed and verified live this cycle. **#92 does NOT advance, and that is the honest outcome, not a stall.** I migrated `run_crypto_portfolio.py` off the weld and measured it (`pbo` 0/9 → 6/9 passing; `reality_check` **9/9 → 2/9** passing — the constant was welded *open* on RC, not only shut on `pbo`; survivors 0 → 0), which is real new evidence *against* R0040's stated premise that the sweep "materially loosens promotion" — on this file it is net-**tightening**. It is still not enough to ship: the before/after windows differ (`funding_carry` sh 1.20 → 1.04) so it is **not a controlled A/B**, and **R0017's end-to-end positive control remains broken** (SR_true +0.5 realises −2.32), so nothing certifies that a genuinely good candidate *can* pass. The code was **reverted** and the measurement carried into the dated 08-02 decision. I had also recorded (F0007) that the migration's safety precondition was already met by `tests/validation/test_stepwise.py:115` — **that was wrong**: those are unit tests of the per-candidate statistics, not the end-to-end positive control R0017 names, and conflating the two is exactly how a phantom-edge-critical change gets waved through. Corrected here rather than left standing. **Staleness:** #71 blocked on a principal/panel ruling since 07-26 (4 days) — named again, not silently carried. #91 remains `paged-tier3`, the carry book is still dead and its release is principal-only. #88 due 08-12, unchanged._

_Re-ranked 2026-08-05T20:35Z. **No re-ordering move, and that is the finding rather than a skip.**
`rerank_gaps.py` reports 50 open rows, 3 needing a decision — #70, #74, #75, all DEADLINE-PASSED —
and all three already carry dated exits recorded by the previous cycle (#70 deferred to 08-12 and
escalated to R0358; #74/#75 paired to 08-19). They keep firing because `rerank_gaps.py:107-109`
takes `min()` over every date in a row, so the ORIGINAL missed deadline dominates any deferral
added afterwards. Clearing the flag would mean overwriting the passed date — erasing the fact that
the desk missed it, which is §34's denominator trick applied to time. Left intact deliberately.
**This cycle's work did not originate here**: it came from `max_audit`'s `test-suite-uncollectable`
and ran to the desk's EVIDENCE BASE rather than to any ranked row — run_ci was filing kernel-killed
steps as broken tests (75cdb2c), the suite's own tmpfs scratch was starving the suite (513ba24,
available RAM 189MB→1555MB), and underneath both sat one genuine committed money-path failure
(eb74f48). Ranking rows against each other could not have surfaced that, because the register
assumes its own measurements are trustworthy. **Staleness named, not carried silently:** the last
stamp before this one was 07-30, six days ago._

## 2026-08-26T05:05Z — weekly gap-fixer/wirer cycle (fleet liveness sweep + scheduler + CI repair)

**FINDING OF THE CYCLE — the arrivals collapse was an INFRASTRUCTURE defect, and the desk was
being told to fix it with research effort.** The conversion gauge reported `ARRIVALS COLLAPSED
— 25 raised against a baseline of 158.0/week` and instructed this seat to HUNT HARDER. Measured
over the 7 days to today: **102 headless seat launches produced 27 real digs (28.1% of 96
billable); 56 died on `auth unavailable` before launching at all.** The miners were not hunting
too little — four launches in five never started. It is a clean time-of-day pattern:

| hour UTC | dead | produced | rate |
|---|---|---|---|
| 05:00 | 0 | 3 | 100% |
| 06:00 | 0 | 2 | 100% |
| 07:00 | 1 | 4 | 80% |
| 08:00 | 0 | 2 | 100% |
| 14:00 | 7 | 0 | **0%** |
| 15:00 | 30 | 5 | **14%** |
| 18:00 | 7 | 0 | **0%** |
| 19:00 | 6 | 1 | **14%** |

**Root cause:** every dig row in `ops/crontab.manifest` is guarded by
`systemctl is-enabled quant-<seat>.timer || <run it>`, and ROOT systemd timers exist for those
seats at 14:00/15:00/18:00/19:00 — exactly the exhausted quota window. The guard therefore
suppresses the manifest's OWN early slots (03/05/06/07, the productive window) in favour of root
timers that fire into a wall. Those are the `quant-dataaxis` / `quant-frontier` /
`quant-prospector` units sitting in `systemctl --failed`, and root is the principal's console.

**Repaired in userland** (`ops/install_early_seat_timers.sh`, commit `2ff54e7a`): the manifest's
own early slots re-homed as user timers with the is-enabled guard dropped — same scripts, same
flocks, same logs, same hours the manifest already specifies. dataaxis 05:00, prospector 06:00,
litminer 07:00, frontier 08:00 + 03:00. Shared `/tmp/seat_*.lock` prevents user/root overlap;
every dig is resume-aware, and `organ_catchup` already knows all five by script and re-fires a
mutex loser every 5 min. This also retires the five `organ-stale-frontier-*` defects at cause.
Fence + ratcheted floor: `scripts/check_seat_launch_yield.py`, daily 10:00 UTC.

**Falsifier evaluated rather than assumed:** `brain_env.sh`'s mutex carries a written falsifier
("if brain_mutex.log shows the daily cycle repeatedly deferred behind long digs, this needs organ
priority"). Measured: **7 deferrals in 7 days against 56 auth deaths.** The mutex is protecting,
not starving — it stays exactly as it is, and `MUTEX_DEFERRED` is excluded from the fence's
denominator so correct serialisation never reads as failure.

**#138 (new, CLOSED) — the scheduler backlog was measured by nobody.** `run_manifest_dispatch.py`
had been writing the post-cron-death backlog to `data/manifest_dispatch_state.json`, and **grep
finds zero consumers**. A measurement with no consumer is an opinion, so "216 uncovered" sat in a
file for six days and escalated to no one — while `check_organ_liveness` (hourly), the organ best
placed to notice, was itself one of the dead rows. Fixed in `a6792b29`: twin check moved before
the count (216/228 published vs 201 truly dead — a gauge counting healed rows as sick can never
reach zero); a memory governor (box: 3814MB, **zero swap**, four organs OOM-killed in 24h) that
DEFERS into `pending` rather than dropping; +17 organs, each hand-run first. Dead tokens
**183 → 166**. Consumer wired: `max_audit.check_manifest_backlog`.

**#139 (new, CLOSED) — a syntax error in committed code passed all three gates for 21h.** The
desk-wide CI had been RED on `tests (pytest)` since 2026-08-25T08:56Z. Cause:
`scripts/liquidation_listener.py` carried `await asyncio.sleep(30)` inside a plain `def`
(`_switch_wait` lost its `async`). **ruff, mypy and `pytest --co` were all GREEN on it** —
verified directly against the HEAD file, which ruff reports as "All checks passed!". `await`
outside `async` is not a parser error: CPython accepts it into the AST and rejects it in the
symbol-table pass, so every AST-level tool is blind (including `ast.parse`, if reached for as a
hand-check — it "passes" where `compile()` fails). Collection missed it because the only importer
imports inside a fixture.

- **Gate closed:** `compile (compileall)` added to `ops/gates.sh` AND `run_ci.py` `_STEPS`, ~1s,
  positioned before the 2h test step. Positive control: reintroducing the bug turns it RED.
- **It earned itself immediately, finding two more files that had NEVER been importable** —
  `desks/mt5/side_channels/failure_mining.py` (two kwarg blocks indented +4 after a multi-line
  f-string; in the MT5 desk) and `desks/mt5/side_channels/check_acc.py` (a `#!/bin/bash` script
  with a `.py` extension, swept in by an hourly sync → renamed `.sh`, capability kept).
- **The defect UNDERNEATH, and the reason this mattered:** with the syntax fixed, the four tests
  in `test_liquidation_flush_durability.py` went ERROR → FAIL and exposed that `_flush()` was
  **never atomic** and cleared its buffer BEFORE the write. That is the desk's single
  most-repeated lesson verbatim — *a heartbeat proves the loop is alive, never that the pipe is*
  — the defect that destroyed ~40 days of liquidations behind a perfectly green heartbeat. The
  implementation existed in `31a4969b` and was LOST in a merge that kept its tests: the
  **kept-tests-dropped-code class**, and the SyntaxError is exactly what hid it, because ERROR
  on import never reports the behaviour the test was written to pin. Restored in full (atomic
  temp+replace, buffer cleared only after durability, corrupt archive QUARANTINED not
  overwritten, plus the second `_STATUS` signal). 4/4 pass.
- **One stale test corrected, nothing loosened:** `test_pytest_has_a_default_timeout` demanded
  `timeout_method = "signal"` while pyproject has said `"thread"` since the same commit added
  both — red ever since. The CONFIG is right: SIGALRM does not exist on Windows and the MT5
  execution box is Windows, where `signal` INTERNALERRORs the whole suite on the box that trades.
  The bounded-suite property is carried by `timeout = 300`, still asserted.

**Liveness sweep (STEP 0):** `quant-moat-backup` had FAILED today (exit 2, DEGRADED 5/6,
`execution_tape` ABSENT). Both causes resolved; detector re-run **clears at OK 5/5, drill PASS**.
`quant-external-pipeline` was OOM-killed at 03:44 and self-recovered on its next hourly tick.
Deliberately NOT built: an automated `backups/ → data/` restore path — manufacturing auto-restore
machinery for a crypto-era retired store is negative-EV and risks resurrecting data someone

**Wired this cycle — the eight-gate barrier had judged ZERO candidates for its entire existence.**

**Process lesson paid for again, recorded:** the first version of this entry was written while a
background suite run was live, and the suite's GAP-113 protected-artifact restore reverted

### 2026-08-26T07:05Z addendum 2 — the CI red, and a defect class promoted to ARCHITECTURAL

**#139 UNDERLYING CI RED: 45 → 14 failing tests, and 5 of the 6 causes were one class.**
The full suite ran to completion (80 min) and reported **45 failures**. Re-run after this
cycle's repairs: **14**. What was fixed, and why it is one story:

| # | symbol/behaviour missing | implementation lives in | landed? |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `liquidation_listener._flush` atomic write + quarantine + `_STATUS` | `31a4969b` | tests merged, code did not |
| 2 | `desks/mt5/.../failure_mining.py` (never importable, +4 indent) | — | syntax, hidden by #1's SyntaxError |
| 3 | `build_bars.ParseAttrition` | `6d204306` | **NOT an ancestor of HEAD** |
| 4 | `recommendations.claim_state` / `CLAIM_TTL_H` | `ddc0b562` | **NOT an ancestor of HEAD** |
| 5 | `max_audit._recorder_pause_reason` | `3da91a1d` | **NOT an ancestor of HEAD** |

**FIVE INSTANCES IN ONE CYCLE PROMOTES THIS TO AN ARCHITECTURAL DEFECT** under the
recurring-defect ladder (1 fix → 2 fence → 3 architectural → 4 redesign candidate). The
component that needs redesign is **the merge process**, not any of these files. Cases 3–5 share
an exact, mechanically-checkable signature: *a symbol a test imports from a first-party module,
absent at HEAD, present on a branch that is not an ancestor of HEAD.* The branch that has it can
be NAMED, so the detector is buildable — carded, deliberately not built on the tail of this
cycle (a half-built scaffold is worse than none, and the `compile (compileall)` gate added today
already catches the subclass that cannot even import).

**Case 5 was LIVE, not cosmetic.** The checks calling `_recorder_pause_reason`
(`recorder-scope-shrank`, `tape-recording-stopped`) are at HEAD; the helper that lets them CLEAR
was not. `grep -c RECORDERS_OFF scripts/max_audit.py` returned **0** while `data/RECORDERS_OFF`
is present and PERMANENT under the MT5 mandate — so the desk's own documented ACCEPT path was
welded shut by the fence meant to watch it, and doing the right thing could never clear the
defect. Same shape the desk shipped before (a file-presence gate holding the sterile cockpit
shut over a retired executor's flat book). Grafted by hand, not restored wholesale: HEAD carries
newer max_audit work (`check_manifest_backlog`, added earlier today) that a wholesale take would
have silently reverted — checked with `merge-base`, not assumed.

**Remaining 14, triaged, all pre-existing and none of this class:** `test_desk_memory` (3 — a
backlog of 59 lessons enforced by no test and 4 with broken `enforced_by` paths, L0173–L0176,
other seats'); `test_scheduler_manifest` (2 — **72** committed units absent from the manifest,
first offender `ops/full-pipeline.timer`; my 7 new SYSTEMD rows do not change the count);
`test_check_risk_units` (2); `test_research_allocator_semantics` (2); and one each in
`test_review_fixes`, `test_dependency_caps` (undeclared imports: playwright, mt5desk,
convert_to_hypotheses, run_all_miners), `test_check_private_bisection`,
`test_wiring_and_ratchets` (34 orphans vs a budget of 30), `test_record_suite_run`.

**Two claims corrected this cycle, both because they would have misled the next reader:**
`d236da86` implied the cgroup kill explained the L1.50 coverage stall (it did not — the ratchet
has no producer at all); and the 3 `TestClaimState` failures that looked like new regressions in

remedies — ground genuinely picked clean is a research verdict (L1.25a: escalate, never rest),
seats dying at launch is an infrastructure defect that hunting harder cannot touch, because it
fires more seats into the same wall.

hand. Not done today deliberately — one day of observations is not a rhythm, and re-homing seats

## 2026-08-26 gap-wirer cycle — the cost model, and why nobody saw any of it

**#142 CLOSED 2026-08-26 — ONE FILE, THREE WRITERS, NO SCHEMA: `tick_value` was deleted from all
197 symbols and every account-currency cost on the desk went to zero.**
`desks/mt5/data/universe/universe.json` is written by `fetch_universe.py` (23 syms, HAS
`tick_value`, spread = median of the H1 `spread` column), `expand_universe.py` (244 syms, no
`tick_value`, spread = a `symbol_info.spread` SNAPSHOT stored under a *median's* name) and
`scripts/download_all_symbols.py` (197 syms, same snapshot). Each writes its own field set with
`write_text(json.dumps(...))`; the file's history alternates between them hour to hour. Measured
consequences, all live: `Instrument.spread_cost_per_lot` returned **0.0 for every symbol** —
`tick_value` is the only field carrying a price in the account's currency, and the property's own
docstring exists solely to warn that the quote-currency formula reads a JPY cross ~150× too
expensive; `classify_all` returned **0 usable instruments out of 197**, an empty universe
indistinguishable from one with nothing worth trading (WS-005); `median_spread_pts` flipped
between 12 and 0 for EURUSD, so the same candidate was uneconomic or free depending on which
producer ran last; and because cost is part of sleeve identity, each flip broke live forward
clocks **terminally** (11 were `IDENTITY_BROKEN` on `cost_hash`). **FIXED** (`f7fe3194`,
`63e4b48d`): `mt5desk/universe_registry.py` is the one writer — merge-never-clobber with per-field
provenance, a degenerate reading (`bars: 0` beside a 50k-row parquet) may not overwrite a real
value, and `tick_value` is derived rather than left absent. The account currency is **EUR** and was
MEASURED not assumed: the derivation reproduces all 19 broker-reported tick_values within 2% across
metals, JPY crosses, CHF, CAD and USD pairs. **0/197 → 197/197 usable, 0 → 115 costable.**
**SELF-HEALING** because all three producers run on Windows boxes with no code-sync path from here:
`quant-universe-registry.timer` re-merges every 20 min, artifact
`desks/mt5/reports/universe_registry_repair.json`. **RESIDUAL, visible not papered over:** 82
equity/index CFDs whose denomination is knowable only from MetaTrader5's `currency_profit`, which
no producer records; `quote_currency()` prefers that field the moment one does.

**#143 CLOSED 2026-08-26 — NOTHING HAS EVER RUN THE MT5 DESK'S 551 TESTS.**
`grep -rl "desks/mt5/tests"` over the repo, `ops/gates.sh`, `ops/githooks` and every systemd unit
returns **nothing**. `testpaths = ["tests"]`; gates runs `pytest --co -q tests/`; ruff and mypy
both list `desks/mt5` in `exclude`. The desk holding the shadow engine, promoter, risk sizer and
gauntlet is outside every gate this repo has — **which is the reason #142, #144 and #145 all
survived**. Measured: 551 tests, 521 pass, **30 fail**, including 11 `test_risk_units` red because
live position sizing could not price a stop in account currency. **FIXED** (`8a0f0668`):
`scripts/check_mt5_suite.py` + `quant-mt5-suite.timer`, 4×/day. A **ratchet, not a blocking gate** —
30 reds as a hard gate blocks every push desk-wide (R0688). The floor is a **set of test NAMES**,
not a count: two identical runs returned 30 and 31, so `>` alarms on noise, and a count is blind to
a SWAP (one fixed while another breaks reads as "no change"). Positive control run both directions.

**#144 CLOSED 2026-08-26 — THE ENGINE CHARGED JPY-CROSS COMMISSION AT 1/184th, AND GOLD AT 3% OF
ITS SPREAD.** `run_backtest` charges `per_oz_roundtrip() / contract_oz` as a price-unit cost.
`spread_per_lot` round-trips correctly; `commission_per_lot` is a CURRENCY amount, so dividing it
by `contract_size` asserts one unit of account currency IS one unit of price — true only for a
symbol quoted in the account's own currency. On this EUR account one yen of CADJPY price is worth
0.005418 EUR, so a 7.00 EUR round turn is 0.01292 yen and the engine charged 0.00007. Separately,
`shadow_forward.per_symbol_costs` still passed `0.48 if sym == "XAUUSD"` — dollars per OUNCE into a
per-LOT field — so gold accrued forward evidence very nearly spread-free. **The real defect is the
shape:** `Costs.from_symbol` was written as the fix for both and the money path hand-rolled the
arithmetic beside it, so the one call site producing forward evidence received neither. GAP 110's
residual. **FIXED** (`d85c35f3`): `quote_per_account`, defaulting to 1.0 so no existing call site
moves silently; the regression test asserts on the AST of `per_symbol_costs` that it calls the
sanctioned constructor and computes no spread of its own. A test pins that the fix can only ever
RAISE a cost, so it cannot manufacture a survivor. **STILL OPEN:** ~35 other hand-rolled
`Costs(...)` call sites keep the 1.0 default and are therefore still uncorrected for non-EUR-quoted
symbols. **PLAN: sweep them to `from_symbol` next cycle** — the highest-EV item left from this one.

**#145 CLOSED 2026-08-26 — A PRODUCER WROTE EVERY BAR FILE TZ-NAIVE AND THE LOOK-AHEAD GUARD
STOPPED GUARDING.** `download_all_symbols.py` uses `pd.to_datetime(..., unit="s")` with no
`utc=True`, and rewrote all 197 H1 parquets over 24 that had carried `datetime64[ms, UTC]`.
`test_day_states_lookahead` — the guard proving no label is computed from future bars — began
raising `Cannot compare tz-naive and tz-aware datetime-like objects` instead of checking anything.
A guard that errors is a guard that never ran (L1.49), and per #143 nothing was running it.
**VERIFIED THE STAMPS DID NOT MOVE** before touching anything: across −4h..+4h the best alignment
with the old bars is **+0h** with essentially complete overlap, so this is a lost LABEL, not a
shifted clock — which mattered, because this desk's entire live family is session-scoped.
**FIXED IN ONE PLACE, NOT 197** (`abffd3fc`): `mt5desk.families._h1` is the de-facto bar
normalizer (154 call sites) and normalized everything except the clock; naive is now LOCALIZED,
the same label-do-not-move operation `h1_source` already applies. The new #143 ratchet **caught my
own change one run later** (8 `test_no_lookahead` failures from a fixture that builds NAIVE bars,
a shape production never feeds it); fixture corrected to match production, and that exposed
`run_backtest` calling `df.index.to_numpy()` on a tz-aware index. Suite floor 30 → 28.

**#146 CLOSED 2026-08-26 — 11 BROKEN FORWARD CLOCKS HAD NO RESTART PATH, AND THAT IS THE READINESS
BLOCKER ITSELF.** `migrate_identity_venue.py` selects rows by `identity_schema != CURRENT_SCHEMA`
and merely COUNTS the `IDENTITY_BROKEN` ones. With 11 sleeves broken on the CURRENT schema it
printed **"nothing to migrate" and exited 0** — while `check_live_readiness` blocked rung 0 on

the verdict branch is gated on `status == "ACTIVE"`); evidence archived never deleted, new window
inherits nothing; restarts counted per key so a repeat drifter cannot look permanently young.
**EXECUTION IS A CONTABO-SIDE ACT** — this box is a read-only replica (`vps_writer_disabled: true`,
`quant-desk-pull` refreshes these files every ~2 min). Report mode runs here and names every stuck
clock. **PLAN: run `--restart-broken` on the trading box once the cost model has held stable for a
full engine pass** — restarting before that would just re-break them.

**#147 OPEN (principal console) — three root units are dead and this box cannot restart them.**

## 2026-08-27 gap-wirer cycle — the forward clock, the cost gates, and the wiring that was never in git

| 162 | **CLOSED 2026-08-27 — the desk's entire forward book was re-based to day zero three times in 32 hours, and nothing measured the one number the path to live capital turns on** | Every promotion on this desk requires `days >= 14` of pre-registered forward evidence (L1.58). The committed history of `desks/mt5/data/sleeve_registry.json` says no clock has ever survived a day: **08-26 02:02 all 15 rows frozen 08-26T01:42; 08-26 08:50 all 15 frozen 08-26T08:49; 08-27 02:10 all 15 frozen 08-27T01:13; 08-27 09:25 all 17 frozen 08-27T03:31-03:34.** Three complete re-bases, none archived anywhere. Meanwhile `monitor_mt5_shadow_sync` reported `status: OPERATING, defects: []` and `live_readiness.json` named the blocker as *"the market has not yet supplied the unseen observations, and nothing can substitute for them"* — a desk defect attributed to the world, and the read that has been steering the desk's assessment of its own readiness. **MECHANISM:** `sleeve_registry._read` returned `{}` for BOTH "no registry yet" and "registry unreadable"; `freeze()` reads that `{}`, finds no row, takes its create branch, mints `frozen_at = now` and a fresh `forward_start`, and writes back a registry holding only the rows frozen in that pass. ONE failed read re-bases the whole book. Its idempotence docstring — *"a caller cannot re-base a running clock by calling this again"* — is true only while the file reads correctly, and false in exactly the case that matters. The read fails for ordinary reasons: the authoritative copy is on the Windows box while `ops/pull_desk_state.sh` scp's this path every ~2 minutes and `freeze()` wrote it non-atomically, so a reader sees a truncated file (ValueError) or an open handle (PermissionError). Both were swallowed into a clean empty verdict — WS-005. | **FIXED** (`e3150633`): `_read` raises `RegistryUnreadable` when the file EXISTS but will not parse; `FileNotFoundError` alone stays legitimately empty. `shadow_forward` already wraps every registry call in `except Exception`, so a corrupt read now skips the row and leaves the running clock untouched — refusing to write is the only safe response to an unknown base. All three write paths go through `os.replace`, removing the CAUSE rather than only the response. Positive control against the pre-fix module: a 14-day-old clock, one truncated read, `freeze()` returns normally and the boundary moves to `now`. **FENCE:** `scripts/check_forward_clock_ratchet.py` + `quant-forward-clock.timer` (30 min) — a boundary may only move EARLIER; later WITH a recorded restart is reported with the days it discarded; later with nothing recorded is a SILENT_REBASE breach that is NOT absorbed into the floor, so it cannot become the new normal. No clocks is UNMEASURED (exit 2), never a pass. First run: 18 clocks, oldest 4.05d, 9.95d to the bar. **RECOVERY REFUSED ON EVIDENCE:** every current `sleeve_id` appears in only two registry snapshots and its earliest recorded `forward_start` is `null`, so the pre-08-27 rows held DIFFERENT identities (the 08-26 cost-model repair, #142) and no earlier boundary is recoverable. Restoring one would be laundering; the clock correctly starts now. | gap-wirer | 08-27 | closed |
| 163 | **CLOSED 2026-08-27 — the x3 cost-stress gate was testing CADJPY at 0.36x, and the gauntlet that decides certification undercharged USDJPY by 184x** | GAP 144 left ~35 hand-rolled `Costs(...)` call sites and named the sweep as its highest-EV residual. Two are on the path that decides who gets a ten-gate certificate. **(1)** `universal_gate`, `run_hunt12/17` and `ug_remote` each rebuilt `Costs(...)` POSITIONALLY from three fields of an existing cost model, so the fourth — `quote_per_account` — reverted to its 1.0 default and un-did the account-currency conversion the baseline had applied. Measured on CADJPY: baseline round trip **1699.29**, "x3" as written **607.00**, x3 correct **1899.29**. The gate whose purpose is proving a candidate survives THREE TIMES its costs was proving it survives a third of them, on the JPY crosses where the live family sits. The 1.0 default exists so adding the field moved no call site — right for a construction, exactly wrong for a RE-derivation, where the safe default becomes a silent revert. **(2)** `external_gauntlet.costs_for` still carried `0.48 if sym == "XAUUSD"` (dollars per OUNCE in a per-LOT field, GAP 110/144's original bug, fixed in `shadow_forward` and never swept here), omitted `quote_per_account` entirely, and scaled a CONTRACTUAL commission with market stress.
